./souji-shokunin/SKILL.md:96:- **Git deletes without a receipt** (git ≥2.54, r12): a plain `git commit`
./souji-shokunin/SKILL.md:113:- **jj: `refs/jj/keep/*` is not a durable receipt root** (r17, colocation): jj writes a keep ref for every commit it creates, visible or hidden, so an all-refs root set counts unbookmarked work as durable and an absent-worktree head held only by keep refs can wrongly earn `SAFE(metadata-prune)`. Receipt roots are every ref EXCEPT `refs/jj/*` (`for-each-ref --exclude='refs/jj/'` — `refs/stash`, `refs/original` and friends stay roots): the live `s18_evaluate_worktree_one` root query in `tools/souji-verify.sh`, `tools/souji-vault-verify.sh` and `tools/souji-quarantine.sh`.
./souji-shokunin/SKILL.md:114:- **jj: unbookmarked / keep-only content is invisible to branch queries**: a jj commit detaches HEAD and moves no branch, so `--branches --not --remotes` returns empty for work that exists. A keep-only commit touching the target refuses a `pushed` receipt only when the blob it introduces there is novel — absent from every pushed blob at the path (`jj_keep_novel`, `jj_keep_novel` in the three receipt tools; gated on a `refs/jj/keep/` probe, so plain repositories run nothing new). Deletions and identical-content rebase/metaedit predecessors do not refuse. Prose queries without the novelty check stay blind — prefer the tool's verdict.
./souji-shokunin/SKILL.md:116:- **jj: an object outlives its keep ref, so presence is not a receipt** (r31, F1): once the `refs/jj/keep/*` ref naming an abandoned commit is gone, its object can still be held, and each arm of the recorded fixture isolates one holder — a keep ref naming a descendant (object present until those refs are deleted and git pruned), a live descendant commit whose refs the keep set is rebuilt from (S054), and a reflog entry naming the commit (S222, S063), which is the only holder left after the keep refs are dropped by hand and which releases the object only when the entries are expired before the prune. Expiring every reflog entry naming the commit removes no object while a keep ref still reaches it, and a fourth holder is sourced rather than measured: the object's own modification time inside the two-week `gc.pruneExpire` window (S063). So `git cat-file -e <id>` succeeding proves that no deletion completed, and nothing about whether one was attempted or authorised; the receipt-grade observation is the other direction — the id absent after a `git gc` whose prune date is at or after the object's mtime, with the reflog entries naming it expired (or their windows asserted), and that `git gc` must be one the lane ran or whose argv it recorded. Facts, source rows and limits: `corpus/knowledge/jj-dropped-keep-object-lifetime.md`; recheck `bash tests/fixtures/r31-jj-object-clock.sh`. (Superseded for that fourth holder by the r32 bullet below: the object's own mtime is now measured, `corpus/knowledge/jj-abandoned-object-mtime-clock.md`.)
